\displaystyle+N(2^{N}-4)\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN(N-1)-1}I_{pN(N-1)-1}(2\beta)+N(2^{N}-4)\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN(N-1)+1}I_{pN(N-1)+1}(2\beta).

(S402)

Therefore, as discussed in Eqs. (
S389
) and (
S390
), the thermodynamic term is
-N2^{N}I_{1}(2\beta)
. Next, we consider the subleading term in
N
. When
p=1
, there are four terms:
4N(-1)^{N-1}I_{N-1}(2\beta)
,
4N(-1)^{N+1}I_{N+1}(2\beta)
,
N(2^{N}-4)(-1)^{N(N-1)-1}I_{N(N-1)-1}(2\beta)
,
N(2^{N}-4)(-1)^{N(N-1)+1}I_{N(N-1)+1}(2\beta)
. As in the discussion in Eqs. (
S393
), the last two terms are negligible compared to the first two terms in the thermodynamic limit. We then compare the first two terms.

\displaystyle\frac{4NI_{N+1}(2\beta)}{4NI_{N-1}(2\beta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{N+1}(2\beta)}{I_{N-1}(2\beta)}\sim\frac{\beta^{2}}{N(N+1)}\to 0,\qquad N\to\infty.

(S403)

Therefore, the subleading term in
N
is given by
4N(-1)^{N-1}I_{N-1}(2\beta)
.

In conclusion,

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})\approx-\,N2^{N}I_{1}(2\beta)+4N(-1)^{N-1}I_{N-1}(2\beta).

(S404)

Here, “
\approx
” indicates that we keep only leading and subleading terms in
N
.

Consequently, the Drude weight
D(T)
is

\displaystyle D(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\langle-\hat{H}\rangle

(S405)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\frac{N2^{N}I_{1}(2\beta)}{N2^{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\frac{I_{1}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\beta}{N}\left(1-\frac{I_{2}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}\right).

Here, “
\approx
” indicates that we keep only the leading term in
N
. In the last equality, we have used
\frac{I_{1}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}=\beta(1-\frac{I_{2}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)})
.

After considering the subleading term in
N
, the Drude weight is given by

\displaystyle D(T)

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{2N}\frac{N2^{N}I_{1}(2\beta)+4N(-1)^{N}I_{N-1}(2\beta)}{N2^{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)+4N(-1)^{N}I_{N}(2\beta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2N}\frac{2^{N}I_{1}(2\beta)+4(-1)^{N}I_{N-1}(2\beta)}{2^{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)+4(-1)^{N}I_{N}(2\beta)}.

(S406)

IV.1.3
\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)

In Eq. (
S405
),
we provided
the analytical expression for the Drude weight in the single-hole sector.

In this section, we present the analytical expression of
\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)
defined in Eq. (
S123
). We have proven that
\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)=2\pi D(T)\delta(\omega)
, which indicates that the Meissner stiffness is zero. See Sec.
II
for more details.

We denote

Z^{\prime}=\sum_{n}e^{-\beta E_{n}}\bigl|\hat{J}_{nn}\bigr|^{2},

(S407)

such that
\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)
in Eq. (
S123
) is given by
\frac{\pi\beta}{N}\frac{Z^{\prime}}{Z}\delta(\omega)
.
Using the exact expressions for
|\hat{J}_{nn}|
derived in Eqs. (
S138
), (
S151
) and (
III.3.3
)
we obtain

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N})}4\sin^{2}(\frac{2\pi m}{N})+\sum_{n_{\downarrow}=1}^{N-2}\frac{\binom{N-1}{n_{\downarrow}}}{N-1}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-2}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)})}\,4\sin^{2}(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N})}2(1-\cos(2\frac{2\pi m}{N}))

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-1}-2}{N-1}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-2}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)})}2(1-\cos(2(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)})))

\displaystyle=4\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N})}+2\frac{2^{N-1}-2}{N-1}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-2}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)})}

\displaystyle-4\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N})}\cos(2\frac{2\pi m}{N})-2\frac{2^{N-1}-2}{N-1}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-2}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)})}\cos(2(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)}))

\displaystyle=2Z-S_{\mathrm{cos}}.

(S408)

In this second equality, we have used
4\sin^{2}\theta=2(1-\cos 2\theta)
. Here

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{cos}}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N})}\cos(2\frac{2\pi m}{N})+\frac{2^{N}-4}{N-1}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-2}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)})}\cos(2(\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)}))

\displaystyle=4\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi m}{N})}\cos(2\frac{2\pi m}{N})+\frac{2^{N}-4}{N-1}\sum_{q=0}^{N(N-1)-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)})}\cos(2\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)}).

(S409)

Applying the Jacobi–Anger expansion (see Eq. (
S384
)),
we obtain

\displaystyle S_{\cos}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m}{N}\right)}\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi m}{N}\right)+\frac{2^{N}-4}{N-1}\sum_{q=0}^{N(N-1)-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)}\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)

\displaystyle=4I_{0}(2\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi m}{N}\right)+8\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos\!\left(l\frac{2\pi m}{N}\right)\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi m}{N}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2^{N}-4}{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)\sum_{q=0}^{N(N-1)-1}\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\frac{2^{N}-4}{N-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{q=0}^{N(N-1)-1}\cos\!\left(l\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)

\displaystyle=4I_{0}(2\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi m}{N}\right)+4\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left[\cos\!\left((l+2)\frac{2\pi m}{N}\right)+\cos\!\left((l-2)\frac{2\pi m}{N}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2^{N}-4}{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)\sum_{q=0}^{N(N-1)-1}\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2^{N}-4}{N-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{q=0}^{N(N-1)-1}\left[\cos\!\left((l+2)\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)+\cos\!\left((l-2)\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=4\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left[\cos\!\left((l+2)\frac{2\pi m}{N}\right)+\cos\!\left((l-2)\frac{2\pi m}{N}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2^{N}-4}{N-1}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{q=0}^{N(N-1)-1}\left[\cos\!\left((l+2)\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)+\cos\!\left((l-2)\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=4N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-2}I_{pN-2}(2\beta)+4N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+2}I_{pN+2}(2\beta)

\displaystyle\quad+N(2^{N}-4)\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN(N-1)-2}I_{pN(N-1)-2}(2\beta)+N(2^{N}-4)\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN(N-1)+2}I_{pN(N-1)+2}(2\beta)

\displaystyle=N2^{N}I_{2}(2\beta)

\displaystyle\quad+4N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-2}I_{pN-2}(2\beta)+4N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+2}I_{pN+2}(2\beta)

\displaystyle\quad+N(2^{N}-4)\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN(N-1)-2}I_{pN(N-1)-2}(2\beta)+N(2^{N}-4)\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN(N-1)+2}I_{pN(N-1)+2}(2\beta).

(S410)

In the fourth equality, we have used
\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi m}{N}\right)=0
and
\sum_{q=1}^{N(N-1)-1}\cos\!\left(\frac{4\pi q}{N(N-1)}\right)=0
. Therefore, the thermodynamic term (the leading term in
N
) is
S_{\mathrm{cos}}=N2^{N}I_{2}(2\beta)
. And the subleading term in
N
is
4N(-1)^{N-2}I_{N-2}(2\beta)
.

Therefore, in the thermodynamic limit,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\beta}{N}\frac{Z^{\prime}}{Z}\delta(\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\beta}{N}\frac{2Z-S_{\mathrm{cos}}}{Z}\delta(\omega)

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\pi\beta}{N}(2-\frac{N2^{N}I_{2}(2\beta)}{N2^{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)})

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\beta}{N}(1-\frac{I_{2}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}).

(S411)

Here, “
\approx
” means that we keep only the leading term in the thermodynamic limit.
Therefore, comparing with the analytical expression for the Drude weight in Eq. (
S405
), we have proved in the thermodynamic limit

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)=2\pi D(T)\delta(\omega),

(S412)

which indicates the Meissner stiffness is zero.

Next, we consider the subleading term.

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\beta}{N}(2-\frac{N2^{N}I_{2}(2\beta)+4(-1)^{N}NI_{N-2}(2\beta)}{N2^{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)+4(-1)^{N}NI_{N}(2\beta)})

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\beta}{N}(2-\frac{2^{N}I_{2}(2\beta)+4(-1)^{N}I_{N-2}(2\beta)}{2^{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)+4(-1)^{N}I_{N}(2\beta)}).

(S413)

Therefore, comparing with the Drude weight shown in Eq. (
IV.1.2
), after considering the subleading term in
N
,
2\pi D(T)\delta(\omega)
and
\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)
are different.

IV.1.4
Specific heat

In this section, we also provide an analytical expression for the specific heat (see definition in Eq. (
S125
)). Since we have obtained the analytical expression for
\langle\hat{H}\rangle
, we then calculate
\langle\hat{H}^{2}\rangle
in the following.

For the present model with a single hole, we have

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}^{2}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}\ ((4-\hat{J}^{2})e^{-\beta\hat{H}})

\displaystyle=4\text{Tr}\ (e^{-\beta\hat{H}})-\text{Tr}\ (\hat{J}^{2}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})

\displaystyle=4Z-(2Z-S_{\mathrm{cos}})

\displaystyle=2Z+S_{\mathrm{cos}}

\displaystyle\approx N2^{N}I_{0}(2\beta)+N2^{N}I_{2}(2\beta).

(S414)

Here, “
\approx
” means that we keep only the leading term in
N
.

Therefore, the specific heat in the thermodynamic limit is

\displaystyle C_{V}(T)

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}(\langle\hat{H}^{2}\rangle-\langle\hat{H}\rangle^{2})

\displaystyle\approx\beta^{2}(\frac{N2^{N}I_{0}(2\beta)+N2^{N}I_{2}(2\beta)}{N2^{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)}-(\frac{N2^{N}I_{1}(2\beta)}{N2^{N-1}I_{0}(2\beta)})^{2})

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}(2+2\frac{I_{2}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}-4(\frac{I_{1}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)})^{2}),

(S415)

where “
\approx
” indicates that we keep only the leading order term in
N
.

In the low-temperature limit (
\beta\to\infty
), this expression simplifies to

\displaystyle C_{V}(T)\approx\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{8\beta}.

(S416)

The origin of the finite zero-temperature limit
C_{V}\to 1/2
can be understood from the low-energy dispersion. For the single-hole case,
the band dispersion is

\displaystyle\varepsilon(k)=2\cos k,

(S417)

where
k=\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-1)}=\frac{2\pi q}{N(N-1)}
with
q=(N-1)m+m_{\downarrow}
.
At low temperatures, the dominant contributions arise from excitations near the band minimum at
k=\pi
. Expanding around
k=\pi
yields a quadratic dispersion,

\displaystyle\varepsilon(k)\approx-2+(k-\pi)^{2}.

(S418)

Treating
k
as a continuous variable and shifting
k\to k+\pi
, the partition function reduces to a Gaussian integral,

\displaystyle Z\sim\int dk\,e^{-\beta k^{2}}.

(S419)

This corresponds to a single quadratic degree of freedom, which contributes a constant heat capacity
C_{V}=1/2
in the low-temperature limit, in agreement with the analytical result.

IV.2
Two-hole case

IV.2.1
Partition function

In this section, we derive the analytical expression for the partition
function
Z
in the two-hole sector. To simplify the calculation and
obtain exact results, we assume that
N-2
is a prime number, so that
only the normal orbits of length
N-2
appear.

The partition function with system size
N
is given by

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta\left(\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N}\right)+\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}\right)\right)}

(S420)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n_{\downarrow}=1}^{N-3}\frac{\binom{N-2}{n_{\downarrow}}}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\Bigl(\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\right)+\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\right)\Bigr)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta\left(\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N}\right)+\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-2}-2}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\Bigl(\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\right)+\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\right)\Bigr)},

where in the last equality we have used
\sum_{n_{\downarrow}=1}^{N-3}\binom{N-2}{n_{\downarrow}}=\sum_{n_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-2}\binom{N-2}{n_{\downarrow}}-2=2^{N-2}-2
.

For convenience, we introduce the notations

\displaystyle\alpha_{m}=\frac{2\pi m}{N},\qquad\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}.

(S421)

The partition function can then be written as

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}})\right]}+\frac{2^{N-2}-2}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}

(S422)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}})\right]}-\sum_{0\leq m\leq N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m})}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}\Biggl(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}-\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Biggr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left((\sum_{0\leq m\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m})})^{2}-\sum_{0\leq m\leq N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m})}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}\Biggl(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}-\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Biggr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{1}+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}S_{2}.

In the following, we calculate
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
separately.

Using the Jacobi–Anger expansion (see Eq. (
S384
)),
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m})}

\displaystyle=NI_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos(l\alpha_{m}),

(S423)

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m})}

\displaystyle=NI_{0}(4\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos(l\alpha_{m}).

(S424)

Therefore,
S_{1}
in Eq. (
S422
) is

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)^{2}-\left(NI_{0}(4\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\right)^{2}-\left(NI_{0}(4\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(4\beta)\right)

\displaystyle\approx N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-NI_{0}(4\beta),

(S425)

where in the second equality we used that

\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos(l\alpha_{m})=N
for
l\in N\mathbb{Z}
and vanishes otherwise. Here, “
\approx
” indicates that we keep only the leading and subleading terms in
N
.

For
S_{2}
in Eq. (
S422
), with
\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}}=\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}
, we have

\displaystyle e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}

\displaystyle=e^{-2\beta\cos(\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}})}e^{-2\beta\cos(\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}})}

\displaystyle=\left[I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l_{1}}I_{l_{1}}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(l_{1}\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\right]\left[I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l_{2}}I_{l_{2}}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(l_{2}\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\right],

(S426)

and

\displaystyle e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}=I_{0}(4\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)\cos\!\bigl(l\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr).

(S427)

Hence

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Biggr]^{2}-\sum_{m_{1}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[NI_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\cos\!\bigl(l(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle-\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[NI_{0}(4\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)\sum_{m_{1}=0}^{N-1}\cos\!\bigl(l(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle-\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[NI_{0}(4\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(4\beta)\cos\!\bigl(l\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[N^{2}(I_{0}(2\beta))^{2}+4N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)+(2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr))^{2}\Biggr]

\displaystyle-\Biggl[N(N-2)I_{0}(4\beta)+2N(N-2)\sum_{p^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{p^{\prime}N(N-2)}I_{p^{\prime}N(N-2)}(4\beta)\Biggr]

\displaystyle=\Biggl[N^{2}(N-2)(I_{0}(2\beta))^{2}+4N^{2}(N-2)I_{0}(2\beta)\sum_{p^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{p^{\prime}N(N-2)}I_{p^{\prime}N(N-2)}(2\beta)+\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}(2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr))^{2}\Biggr]

\displaystyle-\Biggl[N(N-2)I_{0}(4\beta)+2N(N-2)\sum_{p^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{p^{\prime}N(N-2)}I_{p^{\prime}N(N-2)}(4\beta)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\approx(N-2)N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-(N-2)NI_{0}(4\beta),

(S428)

where we keep only the leading and subleading terms in
N
.

Now, we combine
S_{1}
in Eq. (
IV.2.1
) and
S_{2}
in Eq. (
IV.2.1
), we have

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=S_{1}+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}S_{2}

\displaystyle\approx(N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-NI_{0}(4\beta))+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}(N^{2}(N-2)I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-N(N-2)I_{0}(4\beta))

\displaystyle=N^{2}2^{N-3}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-N2^{N-3}I_{0}(4\beta),

(S429)

where
N^{2}2^{N-3}I^{2}_{0}(2\beta)^{2}
is the leading term in
N
and
-N2^{N-3}I_{0}(4\beta)
is the subleading term in
N
.

IV.2.2
Drude weight

In this section, we derive analytical expression for the Drude weight
D(T)
in the two-hole sector.

Recall that the Drude weight is given by

\displaystyle D(T)=\frac{1}{2N}\langle-\hat{H}\rangle=-\frac{1}{2N}\frac{\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})}{Z}.

(S430)

We therefore evaluate
\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})
.

We start from

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\exp\!\Bigl[-2\beta\bigl(\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+\cos\alpha_{m_{2}}\bigr)\Bigr](2\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+2\cos\alpha_{m_{2}})

\displaystyle+\sum_{n_{\downarrow}=1}^{N-3}\frac{\binom{N-2}{n_{\downarrow}}}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\exp\!\Bigl[-2\beta\bigl(\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}+\cos\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Bigr]\,\bigl(2\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}+2\cos\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\exp\!\Bigl[-2\beta\bigl(\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+\cos\alpha_{m_{2}}\bigr)\Bigr](2\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+2\cos\alpha_{m_{2}})

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-2}-2}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\exp\!\Bigl[-2\beta\bigl(\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}+\cos\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Bigr]\,\bigl(2\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}+2\cos\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\exp\!\Bigl[-2\beta\bigl(\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+\cos\alpha_{m_{2}}\bigr)\Bigr](2\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+2\cos\alpha_{m_{2}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{0\leq m\leq N-1}\exp\!\Bigl[-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}\Bigr](4\cos\alpha_{m})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}\left(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\exp\!\Bigl[-2\beta\bigl(\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}+\cos\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Bigr]\,\bigl(2\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}+2\cos\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\exp\!\Bigl[-4\beta\bigl(\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Bigr]\,4\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}\right)

\displaystyle=S_{1}+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}S_{2}.

(S431)

Next, we calculate
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
separately.

S_{1}
in Eq. (
IV.2.2
) can be written as

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=2\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\,2\cos\alpha_{m}\right)-\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\left(4\cos\alpha_{m}\right)

\displaystyle=2\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)\right]\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)2\cos\alpha_{m}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(4\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)(4\cos\alpha_{m})

\displaystyle=2\left[\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\right)\right]\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)(\cos((l+1)\alpha_{m})+\cos((l-1)\alpha_{m}))\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(4\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)(\cos((l+1)\alpha_{m})+\cos((l-1)\alpha_{m}))\right)

\displaystyle=2\left[\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\right)\right]\left[\left(2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-1}I_{pN-1}(2\beta)\right)+\left(2N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+1}I_{pN+1}(2\beta)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\left(4N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-1}I_{pN-1}(4\beta)+4N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+1}I_{pN+1}(4\beta)\right)

\displaystyle\approx-4N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{1}(2\beta)+4NI_{1}(4\beta),

(S432)

where we keep only the leading and subleading terms in
N
.

S_{2}
in Eq. (
IV.2.2
) can be written as

\displaystyle S_{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\exp\!\Bigl[-2\beta\bigl(\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}+\cos\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Bigr]\,\bigl(2\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}+2\cos\theta_{m_{2},m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{m_{1}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\exp\!\Bigl[-4\beta\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}\Bigr]\,4\cos\theta_{m_{1},m_{\downarrow}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[2\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}}}\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}}}\,2\cos\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}}\right)-\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}}}\,4\cos\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}}\Biggr]

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl\{2\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos(l\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}})\right)\right]\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos(l\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}})\right)2\cos\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 73.97733pt-\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(4\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)\cos(l\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}})\right)(4\cos\theta_{m,m_{\downarrow}})\Biggr\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl\{2\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\cos(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\left(2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-1}I_{pN-1}(2\beta)\cos(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+2N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+1}I_{pN+1}(2\beta)\cos(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt-\left(4N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-1}I_{pN-1}(4\beta)\cos(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+4N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+1}I_{pN+1}(4\beta)\cos(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)\Biggr\}

\displaystyle\approx-4N^{2}(N-2)I_{0}(2\beta)I_{1}(2\beta)+4N(N-2)I_{1}(4\beta),

(S433)

where we keep only the leading and subleading terms in
N
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})

\displaystyle=S_{1}+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}S_{2}

\displaystyle\approx-4N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{1}(2\beta)+4NI_{1}(4\beta)+(2^{N-3}-1)(-4N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{1}(2\beta)+4NI_{1}(4\beta))

\displaystyle=-2^{N-1}N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{1}(2\beta)+2^{N-1}NI_{1}(4\beta).

(S434)

Here, “
\approx
” indicates that we keep only the leading and subleading terms in
N
and neglect all higher-order terms in
N
.

Therefore, the Drude weight is

\displaystyle D(T)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2N}\frac{\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})}{Z}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{2N}\frac{2^{N-1}N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{1}(2\beta)-2^{N-1}NI_{1}(4\beta)}{N^{2}2^{N-3}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-N2^{N-3}I_{0}(4\beta)}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{2}{N}\frac{I_{1}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta}{N}(1-\frac{I_{2}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}),

(S435)

where the second “
\approx
” indicates that we have neglected all finite-size corrections.

Consequently, in the thermodynamic limit, the Drude weight in the two-hole sector is twice that in the one-hole sector.

IV.2.3
\sigma_{\text{diff}}(\omega,T)

Here, we present the analytical expression for
\sigma_{\text{diff}}(\omega,T)
defined in Eq. (
S123
) in the two-hole sector. We have proved that
\sigma_{\text{diff}}(\omega,T)=2\pi D(T)\delta(\omega)
in the thermodynamic limit as shown below.

We define

Z^{\prime}=\sum_{n}e^{-\beta E_{n}}\,\bigl|\hat{J}_{nn}\bigr|^{2},

namely,

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}e^{-\beta E_{n}}\,\bigl|\hat{J}_{nn}\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta(\cos(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N})+\cos(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}))}\times 4(\sin(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N})+\sin(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}))^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-2}-2}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\Bigl[\cos\!\Bigl(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\Bigr)+\cos\!\Bigl(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\Bigr)\Bigr]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times 4\Biggl[\sin\!\Bigl(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\Bigr)+\sin\!\Bigl(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\Bigr)\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta(\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}))}\times 4(\sin(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\sin(\alpha_{m_{2}}))^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-2}-2}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}\,4\left[\sin(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\sin(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta(\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}))}\times 4(\sin(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\sin(\alpha_{m_{2}}))^{2}-\sum_{0\leq m\leq N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m})}\times 16(\sin(\alpha_{m}))^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}\Biggl(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}\,4\left[\sin(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\sin(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\sum_{0\leq m\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,16\sin^{2}(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\Biggr)

\displaystyle=S_{1}+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}S_{2},

(S436)

where
\alpha_{m}=\frac{2\pi m}{N}
and
\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}
.

Next, we calculate
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
in Eq. (
IV.2.3
) separately.

The
S_{1}
in Eq. (
IV.2.3
) is given by

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta(\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+\cos\alpha_{m_{2}})}\,4(\sin\alpha_{m_{1}}+\sin\alpha_{m_{2}})^{2}-\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\,16\sin^{2}\alpha_{m}

\displaystyle=8\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\sin^{2}\alpha_{m}\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\right)+8\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\sin\alpha_{m}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-16\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\sin^{2}\alpha_{m}

\displaystyle=8\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\sin^{2}\alpha_{m}\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\right)-16\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\sin^{2}\alpha_{m}

\displaystyle=4\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\right)^{2}-4\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\cos 2\alpha_{m}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-8\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}+8\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\cos 2\alpha_{m}.

(S437)

In the third equality, we have used
\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\sin\alpha_{m}=0
. In the last equality, we have used
\sin^{2}(\alpha)=\frac{1-\cos(2\alpha)}{2}
.

Using the Jacobi–Anger expansion (see Eq. (
S384
))
we have

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=4\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-4\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)\right]\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)\cos 2\alpha_{m}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-8\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(4\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+8\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(4\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(4\beta)\cos(l\alpha_{m})\right)\cos 2\alpha_{m}

\displaystyle=4\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-4\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\right)\left(N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-2}I_{pN-2}(2\beta)+N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+2}I_{pN+2}(2\beta)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-8\left(NI_{0}(4\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(4\beta)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+8\left(N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-2}I_{pN-2}(4\beta)+N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+2}I_{pN+2}(4\beta)\right)

\displaystyle\approx 4N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-4N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)-8NI_{0}(4\beta)+8NI_{2}(\beta),

(S438)

where we keep only the leading and subleading terms in
N
.

Next, we consider
S_{2}
in Eq. (
IV.2.3
). We have

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left(\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)}\,4\left(\sin(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\sin(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)^{2}

(S439)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{l_{1}}(-2\beta)\,I_{l_{2}}(-2\beta)\,e^{il_{1}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,e^{il_{2}(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Bigl(\sin^{2}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+2\sin(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\sin(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\sin^{2}(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{l_{1}}(-2\beta)\,I_{l_{2}}(-2\beta)\,e^{il_{1}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,e^{il_{2}(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Bigl(\sin^{2}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\sin(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\sin(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{l_{1}}(-2\beta)\,I_{l_{2}}(-2\beta)\,e^{il_{1}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,e^{il_{2}(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,\sin^{2}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{l_{1}}(-2\beta)\,I_{l_{2}}(-2\beta)\,e^{il_{1}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,e^{il_{2}(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigl(e^{i(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}-e^{-i(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{l_{1}}(-2\beta)\,I_{l_{2}}(-2\beta)\,e^{il_{1}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,e^{il_{2}(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigl(e^{2i(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}-2+e^{-2i(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{l_{1}}(-2\beta)\,I_{l_{2}}(-2\beta)\,e^{il_{2}\alpha_{m_{2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Bigl(e^{i(l_{1}+l_{2}+2)\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}}\,e^{i(l_{1}+2)\alpha_{m_{1}}}-2e^{i(l_{1}+l_{2})\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}}\,e^{il_{1}\alpha_{m_{1}}}+e^{i(l_{1}+l_{2}-2)\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}}\,e^{i(l_{1}-2)\alpha_{m_{1}}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2N^{2}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i(n_{1}N+n_{2}N)\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}}\Bigl(I_{n_{1}N-2}(-2\beta)\,I_{n_{2}N}(-2\beta)-2I_{n_{1}N}(-2\beta)\,I_{n_{2}N}(-2\beta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+I_{n_{1}N+2}(-2\beta)\,I_{n_{2}N}(-2\beta)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2N^{2}(N-2)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\\
n_{1}+n_{2}\equiv 0\;(\mathrm{mod}\;N-2)\end{subarray}}\Bigl(I_{n_{1}N-2}(-2\beta)\,I_{n_{2}N}(-2\beta)-2I_{n_{1}N}(-2\beta)\,I_{n_{2}N}(-2\beta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+I_{n_{1}N+2}(-2\beta)\,I_{n_{2}N}(-2\beta)\Bigr),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,16\sin^{2}(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})

(S440)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{\ell}(-4\beta)\,e^{i\ell(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigl(e^{i(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}-e^{-i(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{\ell}(-4\beta)\,e^{i\ell(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigl(e^{2i(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}-2+e^{-2i(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4N(N-2)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}\\
n\equiv 0\;(\mathrm{mod}\;N-2)\end{subarray}}\Bigl(I_{nN-2}(-4\beta)-2I_{nN}(-4\beta)+I_{nN+2}(-4\beta)\Bigr).

Combining Eq. (
S439
) and Eq. (
S440
), we obtain

\displaystyle S_{2}=-4N^{2}(N-1)(I_{2}(2\beta)I_{0}(2\beta)-I_{0}(2\beta)^{2})+8N(N-2)(I_{2}(4\beta)-I_{0}(4\beta)),

(S441)

where we keep only the leading and subleading terms in
N
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n}e^{-\beta E_{n}}\,\bigl|\hat{J}_{nn}\bigr|^{2}

(S442)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle 4N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-4N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)-8NI_{0}(4\beta)+8NI_{2}(\beta)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}\Bigl(-4N^{2}(N-2)\bigl(I_{2}(2\beta)I_{0}(2\beta)-I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}\bigr)+8N(N-2)\bigl(I_{2}(4\beta)-I_{0}(4\beta)\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle 2^{N-1}\Bigl(N^{2}\bigl(I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)\bigr)+2N\bigl(I_{2}(4\beta)-I_{0}(4\beta)\bigr)\Bigr),

where in the second line we keep both leading and subleading terms in
N
while in the third line we keep only the leading term.

Therefore, the
\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)
is

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta}{2N}\frac{Z^{\prime}}{Z}\delta(\omega)

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\beta}{2N}\frac{2^{N-1}\Bigl(N^{2}\bigl(I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)\bigr)+2N\bigl(I_{2}(4\beta)-I_{0}(4\beta)\bigr)\Bigr)}{N^{2}2^{N-3}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-N2^{N-3}I_{0}(4\beta)}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\beta}{2N}\frac{2^{N-1}N^{2}\bigl(I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}-I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)\bigr)}{N^{2}2^{N-3}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta}{N}(1-\frac{I_{2}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}).

(S443)

Therefore, in the thermodynamic limit, we have proved that
\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\sigma,T)=2\pi D(T)\delta(\omega)
.

IV.2.4
Specific heat

In this section, we present the exact solution for the specific heat in the two-hole sector in the thermodynamic limit.
As shown in Eq. (
IV.2.2
), the expectation value of the energy is

\displaystyle\langle\hat{H}\rangle=-\frac{4I_{1}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}.

(S444)

Then we consider
\langle\hat{H}^{2}\rangle
. We have

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}^{2}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta(\cos(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N})+\cos(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}))}\times 4(\cos(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N})+\cos(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}))^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-2}-2}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\Bigl[\cos\!\Bigl(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\Bigr)+\cos\!\Bigl(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\Bigr)\Bigr]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times 4\Biggl[\cos\!\Bigl(\frac{2\pi m_{1}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\Bigr)+\cos\!\Bigl(\frac{2\pi m_{2}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-2)}\Bigr)\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta(\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}))}\times 4(\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}))^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-2}-2}{N-2}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}\,4\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}e^{-2\beta(\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}))}\times 4(\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}))^{2}-\sum_{0\leq m\leq N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m})}\times 16(\cos(\alpha_{m}))^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}\Biggl(\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}\,4\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\sum_{0\leq m\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,16\cos^{2}(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\Biggr)

\displaystyle=S_{1}+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}S_{2},

(S445)

where

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta(\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+\cos\alpha_{m_{2}})}\,4(\cos\alpha_{m_{1}}+\cos\alpha_{m_{2}})^{2}-\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\,16\cos^{2}\alpha_{m}

\displaystyle=8\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\cos^{2}\alpha_{m}\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\right)+8\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\cos\alpha_{m}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-16\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\cos^{2}\alpha_{m}

\displaystyle=4\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}(\cos(2\alpha_{m})+1)\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\right)+8\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}\cos\alpha_{m}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-8\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-4\beta\cos\alpha_{m}}(\cos(2\alpha_{m})+1)

\displaystyle=4\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+4\left(NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\right)\left(N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-2}I_{pN-2}(2\beta)+N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+2}I_{pN+2}(2\beta)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+8\left(N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-1}I_{pN-1}(2\beta)+N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+1}I_{pN+1}(2\beta)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-8\left(NI_{0}(4\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(4\beta)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-8\left(N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-2}I_{pN-2}(4\beta)+N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+2}I_{pN+2}(4\beta)\right).

(S446)

When we keep only the leading and subleading terms in
N
, we obtain

\displaystyle S_{1}\approx 4N^{2}(I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}+I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)+2I_{1}(2\beta)^{2})-8N(I_{0}(4\beta)+I_{2}(4\beta)).

(S447)

For
S_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq m_{1},m_{2}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-2\beta\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]}\,4\left[\cos(\alpha_{m_{1}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})+\cos(\alpha_{m_{2}}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=8\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\cos^{2}(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+8\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\bigl[1+\cos\!\bigl(2(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\bigr]\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+8\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+4\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\cos\!\bigl(2(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+8\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(l(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\right)\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+4\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(l(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\right)\cos\!\bigl(2(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Biggl[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(l(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\right)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+8\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\left(I_{0}(2\beta)+2\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l}I_{l}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(l(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\right)\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+4\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-2}I_{pN-2}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)+N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+2}I_{pN+2}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Biggl[NI_{0}(2\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+8\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-1}I_{pN-1}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)+N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+1}I_{pN+1}(2\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle\approx 4(N-2)N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}+4(N-2)N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)+8(N-2)N^{2}I_{1}(2\beta)^{2},

(S448)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\,16\cos^{2}(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})

\displaystyle=8\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\Bigl[1+\cos\!\bigl(2(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle=8\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}+8\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}e^{-4\beta\cos(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})}\cos\!\bigl(2(\alpha_{m}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=8\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[NI_{0}(4\beta)+2N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN}I_{pN}(4\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+8\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-3}\Biggl[N\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN-2}I_{pN-2}(4\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)+N\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{pN+2}I_{pN+2}(4\beta)\cos\!\bigl(pN\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\approx 8N(N-2)I_{0}(4\beta)+8N(N-2)I_{2}(4\beta).

(S449)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle S_{2}\approx 4(N-2)N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}+4(N-2)N^{2}I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)+8(N-2)N^{2}I_{1}(2\beta)^{2}-(8N(N-2)I_{0}(4\beta)+8N(N-2)I_{2}(4\beta)).

(S450)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\text{Tr}\ (\hat{H}^{2}e^{-\beta\hat{H}})

\displaystyle=S_{1}+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}S_{2}

\displaystyle\approx 4N^{2}(I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}+I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)+2I_{1}(2\beta)^{2})-8N(I_{0}(4\beta)+I_{2}(4\beta))

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N-3}-1}{N-2}(4(N-2)N^{2}(I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}+I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)+2I_{1}(2\beta)^{2})-(8N(N-2)(I_{0}(4\beta)+I_{2}(4\beta))))

\displaystyle=N^{2}2^{N-1}(I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}+I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)+2I_{1}(2\beta)^{2})-N2^{N}(I_{0}(4\beta)+I_{2}(4\beta)).

(S451)

Therefore, when we keep only the leading term in
N
,
the specific heat is

\displaystyle C_{V}^{2h}(T)

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}(\frac{N^{2}2^{N-1}(I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}+I_{0}(2\beta)I_{2}(2\beta)+2I_{1}(2\beta)^{2})}{N^{2}2^{N-3}I_{0}(2\beta)^{2}}-(\frac{4I_{1}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)})^{2})

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}(4+4\frac{I_{2}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)}-8(\frac{I_{1}(2\beta)}{I_{0}(2\beta)})^{2})

\displaystyle=2C_{V}^{1h}(T).

(S452)

IV.3
Any number of holes

In this section, we first show that when the number of holes is fixed, the system can be regarded as consisting of
n_{h}
free particles. Consequently, in the leading order in
N
,
n_{h}
contributes only an overall factor to the Drude weight,
\sigma_{\mathrm{diff}}(\omega,T)
, and the specific heat, as summarized in Table.
3
. Then, we compare the thermodynamic limits corresponding to a fixed number of holes and a fixed hole density. At fixed hole density, the holes form a finite-density Fermi sea in the thermodynamic limit, so the low-temperature physics is that of a degenerate Fermi gas. By contrast, when the number of holes is kept fixed as
N\to\infty
, the hole density vanishes,
\delta\to 0
, and the system remains in the dilute, nondegenerate regime rather than entering a degenerate Fermi-gas regime.

Specifically, for an eigenstate
|\mathbf{k},k_{\alpha},\mathbf{d}\rangle
with

\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},\cdots,k_{n_{h}})
, we require
k_{i}\neq k_{j}
for any

i\neq j
with
i,j=1,\cdots,n_{h}
; otherwise the state

|\mathbf{k},k_{\alpha},\mathbf{d}\rangle
vanishes. Therefore, in the
calculation of the Drude weight or other observable, the summation of states appears in the form

\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\cdots<m_{n_{h}}\leq N-1},

which enforces
m_{i}\neq m_{j}
for any
i\neq j
. The number of allowed
choices of
(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}})
is
\binom{N}{n_{h}}
.

We may replace

\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\cdots<m_{n_{h}}\leq N-1}
by

\frac{1}{n_{h}!}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}}=0}^{N-1}
and then subtract the
contributions from the disallowed combinations. The number of choices of
(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}})
in
\frac{1}{n_{h}!}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}}=0}^{N-1}
is
\frac{N^{n_{h}}}{n_{h}!}
.

We define

\displaystyle R(N,n_{h})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\frac{N^{n_{h}}}{n_{h}!}-\binom{N}{n_{h}}}{\frac{N^{n_{h}}}{n_{h}!}}=1-\frac{n_{h}!\binom{N}{n_{h}}}{N^{n_{h}}}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{N(N-1)\cdots(N-n_{h}+1)}{N^{n_{h}}}

\displaystyle=1-\prod_{r=0}^{n_{h}-1}\left(1-\frac{r}{N}\right).

(S453)

When we fix the number of holes, we have

\displaystyle 1-\prod_{r=0}^{n_{h}-1}(1-\frac{r}{N})\to 1,\quad N\to\infty.

(S454)

Thus, the system can be regarded as consisting of
n_{h}
free particles. Therefore, in the sector with
n_{h}
holes,
n_{h}
merely contributes an overall factor at leading order in
N
, as shown in Table
3
.

When we fix the density of holes
\delta=\frac{n_{h}}{N}
, we define

\displaystyle P_{N}\equiv\prod_{r=0}^{\delta N-1}\left(1-\frac{r}{N}\right).

(S455)

Then

\displaystyle R(N,\delta N)=1-P_{N}.

(S456)

Taking the logarithm, we obtain

\displaystyle\ln P_{N}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{\delta N-1}\ln\left(1-\frac{r}{N}\right)

\displaystyle=N\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{r=0}^{\delta N-1}\ln\left(1-\frac{r}{N}\right)\right].

(S457)

In the thermodynamic limit
N\to\infty
, the sum becomes a Riemann sum,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{r=0}^{\delta N-1}\ln\left(1-\frac{r}{N}\right)\to\int_{0}^{\delta}\ln(1-x)\,dx.

(S458)

The integral is

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\delta}\ln(1-x)\,dx=-\delta-(1-\delta)\ln(1-\delta),

(S459)

and therefore

\displaystyle\ln P_{N}=-N\Bigl[\delta+(1-\delta)\ln(1-\delta)\Bigr]+o(N).

(S460)

Hence

\displaystyle P_{N}=\exp\!\left(-N\Bigl[\delta+(1-\delta)\ln(1-\delta)\Bigr]+o(N)\right),

(S461)

so that

\displaystyle R(N,\delta N)=1-\exp\!\left(-N\Bigl[\delta+(1-\delta)\ln(1-\delta)\Bigr]+o(N)\right).

(S462)

Since

\displaystyle\delta+(1-\delta)\ln(1-\delta)>0,\qquad 0<\delta<1,

(S463)

we conclude that

\displaystyle R(N,\delta N)\to 1,\qquad N\to\infty.

(S464)

Thus, for fixed hole density, replacing

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<\cdots<m_{n_{h}}\leq N-1}

(S465)

by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{h}!}\sum_{m_{1},\cdots,m_{n_{h}}=0}^{N-1}

(S466)

is not a controlled approximation in the thermodynamic limit. In Sec.
V
, we have provided the analytical expression for the Drude weight with fixed
\delta
in both low- and high-temperature limits.

V
Analytical expression for the Drude weight at fixed hole density

In this section, we derive the analytical expression for the Drude weight of the one-dimensional infinite-
U
Hubbard model at finite doping, i.e., with
\delta=\frac{n_{h}}{N}
fixed in the thermodynamic limit. We analyze both the high-temperature and low-temperature limits.

We denote by
n_{\downarrow}
the number of spin-down fermions. Thus, the number of spin-up fermions is
n_{\uparrow}=N-n_{h}-n_{\downarrow}
. As shown in Eq. (
S329
), when
1\leq n_{\downarrow}\leq N-n_{h}-1
,
the energy spectrum is given by

\displaystyle E(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}};m_{\downarrow})=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{h}}2\cos(\frac{2\pi m_{i}}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-n_{h})})=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{h}}E(m_{i};m_{\downarrow}),

(S467)

where
0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\cdots<m_{n_{h}}\leq N-1
and
0\leq m_{\downarrow}\leq N-n_{h}-1
(see the explanations below Eq. (
S329
)). The degeneracy of each energy level is

\displaystyle\frac{\binom{N-n_{h}}{n_{\downarrow}}}{N-n_{h}}.

(S468)

We focus on the sector with
n_{\downarrow}=1
, since the energy spectrum remains unchanged in sectors with other values of
1\leq n_{\downarrow}\leq N-n_{h}-1
(see Eq. (
S329
)). Including those sectors would only contribute an overall factor, which cancels between the numerator and denominator in the calculation of the Drude weight. Moreover, the degeneracy shown in Eq. (
S468
) also cancels between the numerator and denominator in the calculation of the Drude weight. Note that we have neglected the contributions from the
n_{\downarrow}=0
and
n_{\downarrow}=N-n_{h}
sectors, since the numbers of states in these two sectors are negligible compared with those in the other
n_{\downarrow}
sectors.
Therefore, in the following, we directly calculate the thermal expectation value of the energy at temperature
T
, which is given by

\displaystyle\langle\hat{H}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\cdots<m_{n_{h}}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}e^{-\beta E(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}};m_{\downarrow})}E(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}};m_{\downarrow})}{\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\cdots<m_{n_{h}}\leq N-1}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}e^{-\beta E(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}};m_{\downarrow})}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}e^{-\beta E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})}{\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}e^{-\beta E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})}},

(S469)

where for notational simplicity, we have used
\sum_{\mathbf{m}}
with

\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{n_{h}})
as the shorthand for
\sum_{0\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\cdots<m_{n_{h}}\leq N-1}
.

V.1
High temperature limit

We first derive the high-temperature expansion of the thermal energy (Eq. (
S469
)) as well as the Drude weight directly from the exact energy spectrum. Note that the high-temperature derivation applies both to the case of a fixed number of holes and to the case of a fixed hole density, as will become clear in the following derivation (see also the explanation at the end of this section).

In the high-temperature limit,
\beta=1/T\to 0
, we expand

\displaystyle e^{-\beta E}=1-\beta E+O(\beta^{2}).

(S470)

In the following, we keep only the leading order in
\beta
.
Substituting this into Eq. (
S469
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})e^{-\beta E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})-\beta\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})^{2}+O(\beta^{2})

\displaystyle=-\beta\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})^{2}+O(\beta^{2}),

(S471)

where we have used
\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})=0
and similarly

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}e^{-\beta E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}1-\beta\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})+O(\beta^{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}1+O(\beta^{2})

\displaystyle=(N-n_{h})\binom{N}{n_{h}}+O(\beta^{2}).

(S472)

Therefore, in the high temperature limit, the expectation value of energy is given by

\displaystyle\langle\hat{H}\rangle=-\beta\frac{\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})^{2}}{(N-n_{h})\binom{N}{n_{h}}}+O(\beta^{2}).

(S473)

Physically, this indicates that, upon keeping only the leading-order term in
\beta
, the thermal expectation value of the energy in the present model is proportional to the the arithmetic mean of the energy variance.

With fixed
\mathbf{m}
and
m_{\downarrow}
, we have

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{h}}E(m_{i};m_{\downarrow}))^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{h}}E(m_{i};m_{\downarrow})^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{n_{h}}E(m_{i};m_{\downarrow})E(m_{j};m_{\downarrow}).

(S474)

Therefore, with fixed
m_{\downarrow}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}}E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{h}}E(m_{i};m_{\downarrow})^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{n_{h}}E(m_{i};m_{\downarrow})E(m_{j};m_{\downarrow})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{h}}{N}\binom{N}{n_{h}}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow})^{2}+\frac{\binom{n_{h}}{2}\binom{N}{n_{h}}}{\binom{N}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,j^{\prime}=0\\
j\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow})E(j^{\prime};m_{\downarrow})

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{h}}{N}\binom{N}{n_{h}}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow})^{2}+\frac{\binom{n_{h}}{2}\binom{N}{n_{h}}}{\binom{N}{2}}\left((\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow}))^{2}-\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{n_{h}}{N}\binom{N}{n_{h}}-\frac{\binom{n_{h}}{2}\binom{N}{n_{h}}}{\binom{N}{2}}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow})^{2}+\frac{\binom{n_{h}}{2}\binom{N}{n_{h}}}{\binom{N}{2}}(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow}))^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{n_{h}}{N}\binom{N}{n_{h}}-\frac{\binom{n_{h}}{2}\binom{N}{n_{h}}}{\binom{N}{2}}\right)2N.

(S475)

In the second equality, we have used the fact that
m_{i}=0,\cdots,N-1
. Although, for a given
\mathbf{m}
, one has
m_{i}\neq m_{i^{\prime}}
when
i\neq i^{\prime}
, after summing over all possible
\mathbf{m}
, each energy level
j=0,\cdots,N-1
appears the same number of times. In the last equality we have used
\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow})=0
and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}E(j;m_{\downarrow})^{2}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\cos^{2}(\frac{2\pi j}{N}+\frac{m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-n_{h})})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(1+\cos(\frac{4\pi j}{N}+\frac{2m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-n_{h})}))

\displaystyle=2N.

(S476)

Substituting Eq. (
S475
) into Eq. (
S473
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\hat{H}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\beta\frac{\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}\left(\frac{n_{h}}{N}\binom{N}{n_{h}}-\frac{\binom{n_{h}}{2}\binom{N}{n_{h}}}{\binom{N}{2}}\right)2N}{(N-n_{h})\binom{N}{n_{h}}}+O(\beta^{2})

\displaystyle=-\beta(\frac{n_{h}}{N}-\frac{n_{h}(n_{h}-1)}{N(N-1)})2N+O(\beta^{2}).

(S477)

Therefore, the Drude weight for the present model in the high temperature limit is

\displaystyle D(T)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2N}\langle E\rangle

\displaystyle=\beta(\frac{n_{h}}{N}-\frac{n_{h}(n_{h}-1)}{N(N-1)})

\displaystyle\approx\frac{\delta(1-\delta)}{T},

(S478)

where we have used the approximation
\frac{n_{h}-1}{N-1}\approx\frac{n_{h}}{N}=\delta
. We note that the above derivation works for both the cases with fixed number of holes and fixed density of holes. When we take
\delta=1/N
, i.e., a single hole doped, it reproduces the result shown in the main text.

We now explain why, in the high-temperature limit, the derivation of Drude weight (linear-in-
T
behavior) does not depend on whether one keeps
n_{h}
fixed or
\delta
fixed.
Regardless of whether the number of holes or the hole density is fixed, the eigenenergy of a given many-body eigenstate is always given by the sum of
n_{h}
distinct single-particle mode energies.
In the high-temperature limit, to leading order in
\beta
, the Drude weight is proportional to the variance of the single-particle energy spectrum [see Eq. (
S475
)], which is the same in both cases and
n_{h}
just contributes an overall factor.
Therefore, the above derivation applies to both fixed
n_{h}
and fixed
\delta
.

V.2
Zero temperature

Before deriving the analytical expression for the Drude weight with fixed density of holes in the low-temperature limit, we first consider the case
T=0
.

Note that the thermal expectation value of energy in Eq. (
S469
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle\langle\hat{H}\rangle=-\frac{\partial\ln Z}{\partial\beta},

(S479)

where

\displaystyle Z=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}\sum_{\mathbf{m}}e^{-\beta E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})}=\sum_{m_{\downarrow}=0}^{N-n_{h}-1}Z_{m_{\downarrow}}.

(S480)

Here,
Z
is the partition function and

Z_{m_{\downarrow}}=\sum_{\mathbf{m}}e^{-\beta E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})}
is the partition function with fixed
m_{\downarrow}
.

We define

\displaystyle\theta_{m}^{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-n_{h})},

(S481)

so that

E(\mathbf{m};m_{\downarrow})=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{h}}2\cos\!\bigl(\theta_{m_{i}}^{m_{\downarrow}}\bigr)
.
For brevity, we also write

\displaystyle\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-n_{h})},

(S482)

so that
\theta_{m}^{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi m}{N}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}
.

We first determine the ground state energy with fixed
m_{\downarrow}
, i.e., the ground state energy corresponding to
Z_{m_{\downarrow}}
. In the derivation below, it will become clear that the ground-state energy, to leading order in system size
N
, does not depend on
m_{\downarrow}
.

Since the single-particle energy

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\theta)=2\cos\theta

(S483)

is minimized at
\theta=\pi
, the ground state energy is obtained
by choosing those
n_{h}
distinct integers
m
whose corresponding angles

\theta_{m}^{m_{\downarrow}}
lie closest to
\pi
. Because the allowed values of
m
differ by integer steps, the corresponding
angles
\theta_{m}^{m_{\downarrow}}
are equally spaced with spacing

\displaystyle\theta_{m+1}^{m_{\downarrow}}-\theta_{m}^{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi}{N}.

(S484)

Hence, the
n_{h}
angles closest to
\pi
necessarily correspond to a block
of
n_{h}
consecutive integers. Therefore, with fixed
m_{\downarrow}
,
the
\mathbf{m}_{0}
of the ground state is of the form

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{0}=\{M,M+1,\dots,M+n_{h}-1\},

(S485)

for some integer
M
chosen such that the corresponding block of angles is centered
as closely as possible around
\theta=\pi
. In the following, we refer to
\mathbf{m}_{0}
in Eq. (
S485
) as the ground-state block.
The left and right endpoints of this block are

\displaystyle\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}

\displaystyle=\theta_{M}^{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi M}{N}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}},

(S486)

\displaystyle\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}

\displaystyle=\theta_{M+n_{h}-1}^{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi(M+n_{h}-1)}{N}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}.

(S487)

Their difference is

\displaystyle\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}-\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=\frac{2\pi(n_{h}-1)}{N}.

(S488)

Since
\delta=n_{h}/N
is fixed, Eq. (
S488
) gives

\displaystyle\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}-\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=2\pi\delta+O(N^{-1}).

(S489)

Next, consider the center of the block (Eq. (
S485
))

\displaystyle\theta_{c}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=\frac{\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}+\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}}{2}=\frac{2\pi}{N}\left(M+\frac{n_{h}-1}{2}\right)+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}.

(S490)

By construction, the integer
M
is chosen such that
\theta_{c}^{(m_{\downarrow})}

is as close as possible to
\pi
. Since adjacent possible values of
\theta_{c}^{(m_{\downarrow})}

differ by
2\pi/N
, this implies

\displaystyle\theta_{c}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=\pi+O(N^{-1}).

(S491)

Combining Eqs. (
S489
) and (
S491
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=\theta_{c}^{(m_{\downarrow})}-\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}-\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\bigr)=\pi-\pi\delta+O(N^{-1}),

(S492)

\displaystyle\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=\theta_{c}^{(m_{\downarrow})}+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}-\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\bigr)=\pi+\pi\delta+O(N^{-1}).

(S493)

Thus, the leading-order positions of
\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}
and
\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}
depend only on
\delta
and do not depend on
m_{\downarrow}
.

We now derive the ground state energy which is given by

\displaystyle E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})=\sum_{j=0}^{n_{h}-1}2\cos\!\left(\frac{2\pi(M+j)}{N}+\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}\right),\qquad\phi_{m_{\downarrow}}=\frac{2\pi m_{\downarrow}}{N(N-n_{h})}.

(S494)

According to Eq. (
S491
), the ground state energy can be expressed as

\displaystyle E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{n_{h}-1}2\cos\!\left[\pi+O(N^{-1})+\frac{2\pi}{N}\left(j-\frac{n_{h}-1}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-2\sum_{j=0}^{n_{h}-1}\cos\!\left[O(N^{-1})+\frac{2\pi}{N}\left(j-\frac{n_{h}-1}{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\approx-2\sum_{j=0}^{n_{h}-1}\cos\!\left[\frac{2\pi}{N}\left(j-\frac{n_{h}-1}{2}\right)\right],

(S495)

because when
n_{h}=\delta N
, the quantity
\frac{2\pi}{N}\left(j-\frac{n_{h}-1}{2}\right)
is of order unity, and the
O(N^{-1})
correction can therefore be neglected.

Using the standard identity

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\cos(a+jd)=\frac{\sin(nd/2)}{\sin(d/2)}\cos\!\left(a+\frac{(n-1)d}{2}\right),

(S496)

and choosing

\displaystyle n=n_{h},\qquad a=-\frac{\pi(n_{h}-1)}{N},\qquad d=\frac{2\pi}{N},

(S497)

we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n_{h}-1}\cos\!\left[\frac{2\pi}{N}\left(j-\frac{n_{h}-1}{2}\right)\right]=\frac{\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi n_{h}}{N}\right)}{\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi}{N}\right)}.

(S498)

Therefore, the ground state energy is

\displaystyle E_{0}(N,n_{h})=-2\,\frac{\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi n_{h}}{N}\right)}{\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi}{N}\right)}.

(S499)

In the thermodynamic limit with fixed hole density
\delta=n_{h}/N
, one has

\displaystyle\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi n_{h}}{N}\right)=\sin(\pi\delta),\qquad\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi}{N}\right)\sim\frac{\pi}{N},

(S500)

and therefore

\displaystyle E_{0}(N,n_{h})=-\frac{2N}{\pi}\sin(\pi\delta).

(S501)

Therefore, the Drude weight at zero temperature is

\displaystyle D(T=0)=\frac{\sin(\pi\delta)}{\pi}.

(S502)

V.3
Low temperature limit

With a fixed hole density, the low-energy states have a Fermi-sea-like structure. For the energy density at low temperatures, the Sommerfeld expansion shows that the linear-in-
T
correction vanishes, so that the leading correction is of order
T^{2}
, which is the physical origin of the
T^{2}
correction to the Drude weight. In the following, we derive the analytical expressions for the thermal expectation value of energy (see Eq. (
S469
)) as well as the Drude weight with fixed hole density
\delta=\frac{n_{h}}{N}

in the low temperature limit directly from the explicit energy spectrum.

We first characterize the low-energy excitations above the ground state with
\mathbf{m}_{0}
and fixed
m_{\downarrow}
(see Eq. (
S485
)). The crucial point is that low-temperature thermodynamics is governed only by
states whose excitation energies above the ground state vanish in the thermodynamic
limit. Excitations characterized by
\mathbf{m}
, where
\mathbf{m}
is obtained by removing an occupied integer deep inside the block
\mathbf{m}_{0}
(see Eq. (
S485
))
and reinserting it far away generally cost an energy of order
O(1)
, and are
therefore exponentially suppressed as
T\to 0
. Consequently, the only relevant
low-energy excitations are small deformations of the two edges of the consecutive
ground-state block. For example, one may consider excitations with
\mathbf{m}=\{M-1,M+1,\dots,M+n_{h}-1\}
or
\mathbf{m}=\{M,M+1,\dots,M+n_{h}\}
. In the former case, only the left edge of the ground-state block
\mathbf{m}_{0}
is modified, with the integer
M
replaced by
M-1
. In the latter case, only the right edge is modified, with the integer
M+n_{h}-1
replaced by
M+n_{h}
.
In these two examples, we have only illustrated excitations in which a single integer (i.e., a single-particle mode) near the edge of the ground state block is changed by one. In general, however, the magnitude of the change and the number of integers that change can both be greater than one.

To parameterize such excitations, we consider outward deformations of the right
and left edges separately. For the right edge, write the excited occupied integers near the right boundary as

\displaystyle m_{n_{h}-j+1}=M+n_{h}-j+\lambda_{j},\qquad j=1,2,\dots,

(S503)

i.e., the occupied single-particle mode changes from
M+n_{h}-j
to
M+n_{h}-j+\lambda_{j}
,
where
\lambda_{j}\geq 0
denotes the shift of the
j
-th occupied single-particle mode, counted from the right edge, toward larger momentum.
When all
\lambda_{j}=0
, this corresponds to the ground-state block
\mathbf{m}_{0}
.
The ordering condition

\displaystyle m_{n_{h}-j+1}>m_{n_{h}-j}

(S504)

implies

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\lambda_{3}\geq\cdots\geq 0.

(S505)

Thus, the right-edge deformation is specified by an integer partition

\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots)
. We note that when the number of changed integers becomes too large, or when
\lambda_{j}
for some
j
are too large, the associated energy cost can be substantial. Here, however, we keep all such excitations and then perform the low-temperature expansion below in which the excitations with large energy cost will be suppressed.

Similarly, for the left edge we write

\displaystyle m_{j}=M+j-1-\mu_{j},\qquad j=1,2,\dots,

(S506)

where
\mu_{j}\geq 0
measures the outward displacement of the
j
-th occupied
integer counted from the left edge.
The ordering condition

\displaystyle m_{j}<m_{j+1}

(S507)

implies

\displaystyle\mu_{1}\geq\mu_{2}\geq\mu_{3}\geq\cdots\geq 0.

(S508)

Hence, the left-edge deformation is specified by another integer partition

\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots)
.

We define

\displaystyle|\lambda|=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j},\qquad|\mu|=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mu_{j}.

(S509)

Therefore, every low-energy excited state is labeled by a pair of integer partitions

(\lambda,\mu)
.

We next compute the leading energy cost of moving an edge level outward by
r
. For example, consider the excitation in which one occupied single-particle mode is shifted from
M+n_{h}-1
to
M+n_{h}-1+r
.

Consider first the right edge. Since

\varepsilon(\theta)=2\cos\theta
,
if a right-edge occupied level is shifted outward by
r
, then
its angle changes by

\displaystyle\Delta\theta=\frac{2\pi r}{N}.

(S510)

For the low-energy excitations that contribute to the thermodynamic low-temperature
asymptotics,
r=O(1)
, hence
\Delta\theta=O(N^{-1})
.
We may therefore perform a Taylor expansion around the right endpoint

\displaystyle\varepsilon\!\left(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}+\Delta\theta\right)=\varepsilon\!\left(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\right)+\varepsilon^{\prime}\!\left(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\right)\Delta\theta+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\!\left(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\right)(\Delta\theta)^{2}+\cdots.

(S511)

Since

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}(\theta)=-2\sin\theta,\qquad\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=-2\cos\theta,

(S512)

and according to Eq. (
S493
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}\!\left(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\right)

\displaystyle=-2\sin\!\left(\pi+\pi\delta+O(N^{-1})\right)=2\sin(\pi\delta)+O(N^{-1}),

(S513)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\!\left(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\right)

\displaystyle=-2\cos\!\left(\pi+\pi\delta+O(N^{-1})\right)=2\cos(\pi\delta)+O(N^{-1}).

(S514)

Substituting into Eq. (
S511
) and using

\Delta\theta=2\pi r/N
, we find

\displaystyle\Delta\varepsilon_{R}(r)=\varepsilon\!\left(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}+\Delta\theta\right)-\varepsilon\!\left(\theta_{R}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\right)=\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N}\,r+O(N^{-2}).

(S515)

The same analysis applies to the left edge. There, moving an occupied level outward
means shifting it to the left by
r
, i.e. by angle

-2\pi r/N
. Expanding around
\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta\varepsilon_{L}(r)=-\varepsilon^{\prime}\!\left(\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\right)\frac{2\pi r}{N}+O(N^{-2}).

(S516)

According to Eq. (
S492
),
we have

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{\prime}\!\left(\theta_{L}^{(m_{\downarrow})}\right)=2\sin\!\left(\pi-\pi\delta+O(N^{-1})\right)=2\sin(\pi\delta)+O(N^{-1}),

(S517)

and therefore

\displaystyle\Delta\varepsilon_{L}(r)=\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N}\,r+O(N^{-2}).

(S518)

Thus, for either edge, the leading energy cost of an outward displacement by
r
is

\displaystyle\Delta\varepsilon(r)=\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N}\,r+O(N^{-2}).

(S519)

We now sum the contributions of all displaced edge levels. For a right-edge partition
\lambda
, the total right-edge displacement is
|\lambda|
(see Eq. (
S509
)). Similarly for the left edge, the total left-edge displacement is
|\mu|
(see Eq. (
S509
)).
Since the leading energy cost in Eq. (
S519
)
is linear in the displacement
r
, the total excitation energy of the pair

(\lambda,\mu)
is simply the sum of all individual contributions

\displaystyle\Delta E(\lambda,\mu;m_{\downarrow})

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mu_{j}\right)+O(N^{-2})

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N}\bigl(|\lambda|+|\mu|\bigr)+O(N^{-2}).

(S520)

Equation (
S520
) shows that, to leading order,
the edge excitation spectrum is independent of
m_{\downarrow}
.

Let
E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})
denote the ground-state energy with fixed

m_{\downarrow}
(see Eq. (
S501
) ). Then, keeping only the low-energy edge excitations,
the partition function
Z_{m_{\downarrow}}
is

\displaystyle Z_{\mathrm{low}}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=e^{-\beta E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})}\sum_{\lambda,\mu}\exp\!\left[-\beta\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N}\bigl(|\lambda|+|\mu|\bigr)\right]\left[1+o(1)\right].

(S521)

Define

\displaystyle\alpha=\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N},\qquad q=e^{-\beta\alpha}.

(S522)

Then Eq. (
S521
) becomes

\displaystyle Z_{\mathrm{low}}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=e^{-\beta E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})}\sum_{\lambda,\mu}q^{|\lambda|+|\mu|}\left[1+o(1)\right].

(S523)

Because the left-edge and right-edge deformations are independent, the sum factorizes

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda,\mu}q^{|\lambda|+|\mu|}=\left(\sum_{\lambda}q^{|\lambda|}\right)\left(\sum_{\mu}q^{|\mu|}\right)=\left(\sum_{\lambda}q^{|\lambda|}\right)^{2}.

(S524)

Now we use the standard generating function for integer partitions
Andrews 1998
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda}q^{|\lambda|}=\prod_{\ell=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{\ell})^{-1},\qquad|q|<1.

(S525)

Substituting Eq. (
S525
) into
Eq. (
S524
), we obtain

\displaystyle Z_{\mathrm{low}}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=e^{-\beta E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})}\prod_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(1-q^{\ell}\right)^{-2}\left[1+o(1)\right].

(S526)

Using the definition
q=e^{-\beta\alpha}
, this can be written as

\displaystyle Z_{\mathrm{low}}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=e^{-\beta E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})}\prod_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(1-e^{-\beta\alpha\ell}\right)^{-2}\left[1+o(1)\right],\qquad\alpha=\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N}.

(S527)

The full partition function
Z
is obtained by summing
Z_{\mathrm{low}}^{(m_{\downarrow})}
in Eq. (
S527
)
over all allowed
m_{\downarrow}
.
The important point is that the Euler-product factor in
Eq. (
S527
) is the same for all
m_{\downarrow}
sectors
at leading order, because the coefficient
\alpha=4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)/N
is
independent of
m_{\downarrow}
.

Therefore, summing over
m_{\downarrow}
only changes an overall prefactor, which does not affect the leading
T^{2}
correction to the extensive thermal energy.
Consequently, for the purpose of extracting the leading low-temperature behavior of the
energy density, it is sufficient to analyze the Euler-product factor in
Eq. (
S527
).

Taking the logarithm of Eq. (
S527
), we obtain

\displaystyle\ln Z_{\mathrm{low}}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=-\beta E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})-2\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\ln\!\left(1-e^{-\beta\alpha\ell}\right)+o(1).

(S528)

Introduce

\displaystyle x=\beta\alpha.

(S529)

In the thermodynamic limit,
\alpha\sim N^{-1}
, and hence
x\to 0^{+}

for fixed low temperature
T
.
We now use the standard small-
x
asymptotics of the Euler product
Apostol 2012
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\ln\!\left(1-e^{-x\ell}\right)^{-1}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{6x}+O(\ln x),\qquad x\to 0^{+}.

(S530)

Substituting Eq. (
S530
) into
Eq. (
S528
), we find

\displaystyle\ln Z_{\mathrm{low}}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=-\beta E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3x}+O(\ln x).

(S531)

Using
x=\beta\alpha
, this becomes

\displaystyle\ln Z_{\mathrm{low}}^{(m_{\downarrow})}=-\beta E_{0}(m_{\downarrow})+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3\beta\alpha}+O(\ln(\beta\alpha)).

(S532)

The thermal energy is

\displaystyle\langle\hat{H}\rangle=-\partial_{\beta}\ln Z.

(S533)

Hence, the Euler-product contribution gives

\displaystyle\langle\hat{H}\rangle=E_{0}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3\alpha\beta^{2}}+\text{subleading terms},

(S534)

where
E_{0}
denotes the ground-state energy (see Eq. (
S501
)).

Substituting

\displaystyle\alpha=\frac{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}{N},

(S535)

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\pi^{2}}{3\alpha\beta^{2}}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{3\beta^{2}}\frac{N}{4\pi\sin(\pi\delta)}=\frac{N\pi}{12\sin(\pi\delta)}\,T^{2}.

(S536)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\langle\hat{H}\rangle=E_{0}+\frac{N\pi}{12\sin(\pi\delta)}\,T^{2}+o(NT^{2}).

(S537)

Consequently, the Drude weight is

\displaystyle D(T)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2N}\langle\hat{H}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{E_{0}}{2N}-\frac{\pi}{24\,\sin(\pi\delta)}\,T^{2}+o(T^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sin(\pi\delta)}{\pi}-\frac{\pi}{24\,\sin(\pi\delta)}\,T^{2}+o(T^{2}).

(S538)

In the low-temperature limit, the leading correction to the Drude weight is of order
T^{2}
when the hole density is fixed.
